The fix is in `src/Space.cpp`, the only file changed.\n\n**I couldn't watch the video.** `/app/.harbor_assets/recording.mp4` isn't in the environment; the folder is empty. So I worked from your description, reproduced the problem in the game, and found the cause there. If the recording shows something other than a takeoff from a pad, tell me and I'll check that too.\n\n**What I reproduced:** I ran the game without a display and launched the player's ship from Cydonia (a ground station on Mars) without autopilot. A few frames after liftoff, while the ship was rising at 2 m/s, the pad registered collisions with it and hull dropped to 99.994%.\n\n**Cause:** the collision code works out which side of a contact the ship is on, then ignores that result. When the station happens to be listed first in a collision, the direction is backwards. A ship lifting away from the pad looks like it is moving *into* the pad, so it takes damage and gets pushed back down. That's why the damage starts before anything visibly hits, and why the launch goes wrong. The bug is in the collision code, not tied to a particular pad, so the fix covers every pad on every station.\n\n**Fix:** that part of `hitCallback` (`src/Space.cpp:979–1014`) now uses the corrected direction for the \"is it moving away\" check, the bounce, and the push-out. When the ship is listed first, the result is exactly the same as before.\n\n**Results after the fix:**\n- **Cydonia, takeoff without autopilot:** hull stays at 100%.\n- **Cydonia, takeoff with autopilot:** hull stays at 100%.\n- **Mars High (orbital station), takeoff with and without autopilot:** hull stays at 100% throughout the 22-second launch window I logged. After that, a ship left climbing at constant speed with nobody steering eventually hits the far side of the station's hub. That's a new crash, not launch damage.\n- I didn't test docking. Landing collisions go through the same code, but I haven't confirmed them.\n\n**Separate startup crash:** the game crashed on startup here, in the star-system cache code (`src/Space.cpp:747`), which has nothing to do with this bug. To run my tests I added a temporary guard there and some debug logging.
